14:T151a,## Project / Coordination

- Project board: https://github.com/orgs/elizaOS/projects/15
- Parent tracker or epic: #20726
- Coordination Discussion: #20726
- Suggested lane tag: /root

## Contribution Provenance

AI assistance is self-reported provenance, not a request for hidden reasoning.

- AI assistance: yes
- AI provider/model: OpenAI / gpt-5.6-sol
- Client / agent tooling: Codex
- Contribution skill revision: elizaOS/eliza@2d89d6b0a9897457ef1a452f0c8bc76684c23a1:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza
- Attribution status: self-reported

## Scope

Add the durable control-plane coordinator that connects an exact dual-verified manifest-v3 source to capacity reservation, old-workload fencing, vault-key seeding, quarantined container creation, streaming restore, Agent generation commit, restart attestation, functional probes, activation publication and catalogue finalization. Start with explicit restore behind an off-by-default gate; automatic node-loss failover and isolated drills consume the same authority in follow-on slices.

## Current progress — 2026-09-06

This is the next active delivery slice of #20726. The former PR review backlog and #30443 dependency have merged; there is no remaining wait for those PRs.

- [x] Merge the candidate foundation stack: #29951, #29957, #29958, #29959, #29990, #29991, #29992 and #29993.
- [x] Merge the capacity-proof teardown dependency #30443.
- [x] Confirm all nine merge commits are included in inspected `develop@de30ecf38c4e64999aa90bcaf8d77b7d8be956a4`.
- [ ] Complete the functional integration and evidence required by the acceptance criteria below.

[Merge and current-source evidence](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/20726#issuecomment-5559596714). The merged pieces provide authenticated streaming, durable candidate/record/seal authority, isolated filesystem staging and character/media/state/vault materializers. They do not yet provide a running restored generation: the quarantined-create runtime returns at `container_created`, and the stream/materializer entry points have no production callers in the inspected revision.

## Next implementation sequence

1. Complete database materialization and assemble all five authenticated components into one isolated candidate behind the concrete Agent restore protocol. Preserve cancellation, bounded streaming, durable receipts and exact replay.
2. Connect that protocol to the durable coordinator after quarantine creation. Commit and restart onto the exact generation; reconcile crashes and lost responses at each effect.
3. Verify restart identity, readiness and signed functional state. Only then commit funding/activation authority and route CAS, followed by exact old-target cleanup.
4. Prove end-to-end replay, lease loss, tamper, node-incarnation changes, capacity saturation and failed publication. Any failed restore keeps routes unavailable and must never become an empty boot.
5. Under separately authorized staging access, demonstrate backup → wipe → restore → boot → functional probe, including database/media/vault/persona/state evidence, a remembered unique fact, exactly one response and measured RTO.

The immediate reviewable deliverable is one complete explicit restore through this path. Automatic node-loss orchestration belongs to #24594 after this path works. Incremental-chain and durable multipart publication work remains tracked by #24601; existing provider primitives should be reused.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A dedicated primary-DB operation/phase machine survives process crashes and response loss at every side effect.
- [ ] The source, lease, copy role, target generation/node incarnation/container/image and job execution are exactly fenced.
- [ ] GC/catalog mutation cannot invalidate a recovery after destructive cutover.
- [ ] The target volume receives the retained vault key before Docker create; secrets never appear in argv, logs or receipts.
- [ ] The Agent seals and commits all five components, restarts onto the exact generation, and emits a signed canonical functional-probe receipt without publishing early.
- [ ] Routes remain unavailable until probe, funding and activation CAS succeed; catalogue verification references the exact probe receipt.
- [ ] Replay, lease loss, node ABA, capacity saturation, tamper, restart response loss and routing tests pass.
- [ ] Feature remains off by default until exact staging wipe/restore proof is attached.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: staging wipe/restore walkthrough posted before enabling; N/A for foundation commits.
- [ ] Logs: privacy-safe phase timeline and retry/fence codes.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: restored QA agent recalls a unique fact and emits exactly one response.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: pseudonymized source/target authorities, hashes, receipts, DB/media/vault/persona/plugin facts and RTO.
- [ ] CI or local commands: PGlite phase/concurrency tests, real Agent HTTP/FS tests, package gates and exact staging run.

## Claim / Status Notes

No production or staging mutation is authorized by this issue. Automatic failover and drills remain fail-closed until their own evidence is complete.

— [/root]

- Contribution skill revision: elizaOS/eliza@2d89d6b0a9897457ef1a452f0c8bc76684c23a1:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza

## Goal

Make Hetzner Robot and Cloud sandboxes recoverable without silent empty boots: authenticated manifest-v3 backups on independent primary/secondary stores, bounded RPO/RTO, durable restore/failover, capacity backpressure, and backup-before-destructive billing/lifecycle operations.

The current state is explicitly **NO-GO** until every acceptance proof below is complete.

## Design doc

- [Agent backup architecture](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/develop/packages/cloud/shared/docs/agent-backup.md)

## Scope

- Agent + Cloud backup format, catalogue, streaming capture/publication and restore
- R2 primary and independent Hetzner secondary storage
- Durable restore coordinator, immutable source/target authorities, vault key seeding, quarantine commit, functional probes and route CAS
- Robot/Cloud node-loss recovery and capacity admission
- Billing freeze/recovery and destructive lifecycle gates
- Scheduler, GC, observability, restore drills and staging evidence

## Non-goals

- Touching production without separate explicit authorization
- Treating cryptographic verification without a booted runtime as a successful restore drill
- Falling back to a fresh/empty boot after any restore error

## Current progress and next work — 2026-09-06

The previous restore foundation stack is merged: #29951, #29957, #29958, #29959, #29990, #29991, #29992 and #29993. The capacity-test dependency #30443 is also merged. All nine merge commits are present in inspected `develop@de30ecf38c4e64999aa90bcaf8d77b7d8be956a4`. [Verified merge and source inventory](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/20726#issuecomment-5559596714).

These merges retire the former review backlog; they do not prove a booted, functional manifest-v3 restore. The current quarantine runtime stops at container creation, and the new stream/materializer entry points still lack production callers.

**Next active work item: #20732 — complete explicit restore before routing.** Connect the candidate database and file materializers to the Agent/coordinator protocol, commit the exact generation, boot and attest it, verify functional state, then publish activation and routes under their existing fences. Keep the feature disabled until the required staging evidence is available.

Existing follow-on work, without creating duplicate issues:

- #24594: automatic Robot/Cloud node-loss recovery, using the completed explicit restore path; includes returning-node and old-placement fencing.
- #24601: bounded incremental chains, compaction and durable multipart publication. The object primitive from #29958 is merged; the durable publication lifecycle is still a separate requirement.
- #24407: fair scheduler admission, cohorts, legacy-cron retirement and 10,000-agent evidence.
- #24123: provision/wake activation and vault-authority writers; retain as an open integration work item until its own criteria are verified.
- #23098 and related PR #30815: lifecycle/billing/deletion coordination. Track their acceptance separately; do not absorb their unrelated scope here.
- Finish the real provider restore drills, measured RPO/RTO/cost and artifacts A–E before overall acceptance.

#20730 and #20735 are closed foundation issues. Broad acceptance rows below remain open wherever operational integration or evidence is still missing. PR merge status alone does not mark these rows complete.

## Work Items

- [ ] 1. Lifecycle and immutable DB authorities
- [ ] 2. Manifest-v3 contracts, record stream and key bundle
- [ ] 3. Catalogue, R2/secondary storage, retention and GC
- [ ] 4. Streaming capture/publication and bounded RPO scheduler
- [ ] 5. Durable restore coordinator, Robot/Cloud recovery and capacity fencing
- [ ] 6. Billing freeze/recovery and backup-before-destructive lifecycle
- [ ] 7. E2E, observability, cost model, deployment and restore drills

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Robot and Cloud node loss both restore DB, media, vault, persona and state files without split-brain or silent empty boot.
- [ ] Verified local-state RPO is at most 15 minutes and unit restore RTO on existing capacity is at most 15 minutes.
- [ ] Primary and secondary copies are independently verified; retention/GC cannot race capture or restore.
- [ ] Capacity floods remain queued/deferred without oversubscription or unplanned autoscale.
- [ ] Every destructive lifecycle and billing freeze takes, uploads and verifies a final restorable backup first.
- [ ] Daily primary and weekly secondary restore drills boot a runtime and verify functional state.
- [ ] All related implementation PRs have independent approvals and applicable green CI for their final reviewed revisions; the original seven-work-item plan is not a fixed PR count.
- [ ] Exact-SHA staging artifacts A-E are posted; production remains untouched.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: staging restore/failover/freeze walkthroughs posted inline; N/A during code-only foundation PRs.
- [ ] Logs: privacy-safe phase timelines, RPO/RTO, fences and cleanup.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: one restored agent recalls a unique acknowledged fact and produces exactly one response.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: pseudonymized operation IDs, hashes, receipts, schema/image identity, inventory and before/after quota/capacity.
- [ ] CI runs: focused tests plus package/root gates on every rebased PR head.

## Dependencies / Sequencing

The seven work items are stacked because later recovery and lifecycle paths consume the immutable authorities, v3 contracts and storage foundations. No stack head may contain a partially applied or subsequently edited migration.

# Sync with develop

- [x] Rebased onto `origin/develop@9399e4596142e429470ba00b3db39a27f43d1686`; zero conflicts.
- [x] Exact-head focused contract/KMS suites and Core typecheck pass post-sync.

# Risks

**Medium.** This adds security-sensitive wire and cryptographic contracts consumed by later backup/restore PRs. It does not activate a Cloud runtime, mutate a database, contact a provider, or change deployment behavior. The main risks are compatibility, parser bounds/cancellation, canonicalization, key-context binding, and secret lifetime; each has focused adversarial coverage.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It defines the runtime-independent foundation for authenticated Goal 3 backups:

- strict manifest v2/v3 contracts with canonical tenant, source, runtime, component, watermark, vault-key, and operation-key-bundle authority;
- the bounded/cancellable capture-v2 frame parser and the canonical `ELZ2REC1` record-stream codec;
- normalized UTF-8 path ordering and filesystem-alias rejection;
- KMS Local and Steward-wire operation-key-bundle wrapping with exact context/version checks and transient-key zeroization;
- replay, tamper, cross-tenant, ordering, deadline, cancellation, ownership, and mutation-after-verification tests.

This PR deliberately contains no Agent capture activation, Cloud runtime, schema migration, provider operation, or deployment wiring. Those remain in later stack PRs.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature: additive Shared/Core contracts and tests.

# Documentation changes needed?

No project documentation change is required for this contract-only PR. The public runtime and operator behavior remain unchanged; stack-level operational documentation lands with the runtime PRs that consume these contracts.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `packages/shared/src/contracts/agent-backup-manifest-v3.ts`
2. `packages/shared/src/contracts/agent-backup-capture-v2.ts`
3. `packages/shared/src/contracts/agent-backup-record-stream-v1.ts`
4. `packages/core/src/security/kms/operation-key-bundle.ts`

## Detailed testing steps

Run from the repository root on this exact head:

```bash
bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts

1a:Td39,Part of #20314

## Outcome

Moves the official Personal Shared Telegram root webhook into the Cloudflare Worker behind a fail-closed gate. This deletes the measured Railway-to-Cloudflare public bounce from the activated path instead of attempting to cache around it. Suffixed per-agent and Dedicated Telegram webhooks remain on gateway-webhook.

The same source supports a migration-safe rollout:

1. Worker baseline: delivery Durable Object and authenticated gateway handoff routes present, edge routing false.
2. Gateway staging: one authenticated handoff per Personal Shared message to that Worker; legacy Redis delivery markers reconcile into the Worker ledger.
3. Worker activation: identical source, edge routing true, so Telegram reaches the Worker directly and the remaining public bounce disappears.
4. Rollback: return only to the post-migration flag-false Worker version; never cross the Durable Object migration.

Production and staging remain disabled until protected live proof.

## Correctness and safety

- Provider webhook signature is verified before delivery state allocation; the gateway handoff preserves and re-verifies the original signature.
- Default turn and LINK routes execute inside canonical Cloud bindings, request/idempotency context, R2, and request-scoped DB cache lifecycle.
- A real PGlite LINK test proves projection invalidation, canonical linked-owner resolution, and identical `already_used` replay healing.
- The Worker ledger uses a token-independent project/account/sender namespace.
- Gateway Redis is migration compatibility state only: new Personal Shared turns have one Worker authority, and old delivered/ambiguous markers reconcile once.
- Delivery state stores deterministic chunk hashes and state only, never message text or credentials.
- Multipart replies persist per-chunk progress. Accepted chunks cannot resend; explicit 403 releases only the rejected chunk; 429 retries only that chunk using bounded Retry-After; invalid responses and transport failures remain fail-closed ambiguous.
- A persistence failure after provider acceptance cannot cause a resend.
- Durable Object alarms physically delete expired state.

## Exact-head gates

Head: `3c18d3a2562390a3bfef8ae0595a2537b8fd896c`
Base: `d8f13f4d3ce528c1748b6bf3e6bb80bbcd2b61cc`

- gateway-webhook: 162/162 tests, 451 assertions
- Worker edge, delivery DO, entrypoint, and real PGlite LINK: 55/55 tests, 265 assertions
- cloud-services-common: 11/11 tests, 33 assertions
- cloud-api, gateway-webhook, and cloud-services-common typechecks: PASS
- production Wrangler dry bundle: PASS; PersonalTelegramDelivery binding present; production gate false
- Biome exact changed paths: PASS
- `git diff --check`: PASS
- aggregate: 228 tests, 749 assertions, 0 failures

## Live proof required before non-draft

Protected staging must record the exact Worker version/deployment and gateway deployment/source, then exercise:

- cold and warm ordinary chats (including “hey how are you”)
- memory follow-up
- duplicate/replay and multipart delivery
- LINK and identical replay
- timed reminder round trip
- at least 30 real messages with provider-to-Worker, runtime/model, and provider-egress timestamps
- p50/p95 comparison against the retained Railway baseline
- flag-false rollback proof on the post-migration Worker version

No production mutation is authorized by this PR.

# Sync with develop

- Last rebase: parent `386fc6afc4cef32a7296d0d8864da95f6a65c412`, producing head `b4a954e17069f10620551288c2faee569f223502`; stable patch-id `30dd217d98bfc7e70939c4eb5279bd893f2be9ef`.
- Current drift: `origin/develop@49a71d7f975d26f8893e44a40b6a51968e0da9c2` is 38 commits ahead of that parent. This PR does **not** claim to be up to date with current `develop`; another rebase and post-rebase validation are required before Ready/review.

# Risks

**Low to medium.** This is a read-only Billing surface. The main risks are rejecting a valid server payload too aggressively, exposing a stale organization snapshot, or confusing an initial load with a retry. Runtime validation, tenant-keyed queries, fresh-organization gating, explicit unavailable/stale states, and the `isFetched` retry fix mitigate these risks. The PR does not mutate payments, credits, quotas, providers, deployments, or backend enforcement.

Non-blocking follow-ups/trade-offs:

- P3: harden `sourcePresentation` against inherited object keys in a follow-up.
- Revalidating organization membership on focus can reset an unsaved local Billing draft; this is the explicit tenant-safety trade-off.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds an **Account limits** section to Billing while leaving payment-setting cards intact.
- Reads only `GET /api/v1/billing/limits`, never sends a client-selected organization id, and strictly validates the response.
- Shows server-reported character, container, app, split sandbox, quota-accounted storage, and inference-cap observations without inventing remaining usage, enforcement status, or reset time.
- Preserves healthy resources when one authority is unavailable and marks cached values stale after refresh failure.
- Covers loading, offline/paused, error, retry, refresh, partial-data, responsive, keyboard-focus, reduced-motion, and light/dark states.
- Fixes retry rendering by using `isFetched`: a never-fetched pending query remains loading, while a previously fetched query with no data renders the retry alert and keeps it busy during refetch.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature (non-breaking, read-only UI addition), 11 files changed.

# Documentation changes needed?

No separate product documentation is required. UI copy names the endpoint's actual limits and states what the endpoint does not report.

# Risks

Medium. This changes proactive-message delivery and retry classification across Shared scheduling, Discord, and Blooio/iMessage. The main risks are duplicate messages after ambiguous provider acceptance, permanently stranded stale claims, and false success without a durable provider receipt. The implementation uses bounded stale-claim recovery, persists and reuses the occurrence idempotency key, distinguishes known provider rejection from unknown acceptance, and fires only after a strictly validated receipt.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Extends the canonical Shared reminder destination from Telegram-only to a server-owned discriminated private-DM contract for Telegram, Blooio/iMessage, and Discord.
- Keeps the canonical scheduler and Cloudflare cron/CAS path; only the existing provider-owned gateway is selected at egress.
- Persists an occurrence-level dispatch idempotency key and reuses it across retries and stale-claim recovery.
- Adds bounded recovery for stale unsettled scheduler claims rather than leaving them permanently stranded.
- Classifies webhook Redis failures before provider egress as retryable/not accepted and keeps claim-release failures from masking the provider result.
- Separates Discord known rejection from acceptance ambiguity; ambiguous sends retain their cursor/lease and are not blindly resent.
- Requires durable provider receipts and validates connector envelopes before recording a reminder as fired.
- Enforces the connector-safe 2,000-character reminder bound at creation and defensively at dispatch.
- Commits names-only rollout/config contracts for `GATEWAY_INTERNAL_SECRET`; no secret values are read or committed.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature plus correctness hardening for a non-breaking Shared reminder delivery surface.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is required. The canonical scheduling and connector ownership contracts remain unchanged.

# Testing

1. `plugins/plugin-scheduling/src/scheduled-task/store.ts`

4. `packages/cloud/services/gateway-webhook/src/internal-delivery.ts`

1f:Te82,## Summary

Completes the remaining #14864 scope on top of the four merged ledger slices (#15086, #15645, #15653, #15699). The triage gap was: `buildCommitmentRegretAudit()` and `trackDocumentObligationArtifact()` had no production callers, BRIEF/PRIORITIZE never consumed the ledger, and no class-level hook installed standing trackers.

### 1. Extraction evaluator (chat + transcripts)
- New `commitment_extraction` evaluator (`src/lifeops/commitments/extraction-evaluator.ts`), registered in the plugin's evaluators, runs in the merged post-turn model call for owner messages only.
- Deterministic false-positive guard: candidate evidence must appear verbatim in the source, carry a first-person commitment cue, not be hedged (`maybe`/`sometime`/…), and clear a confidence floor. A hedged remark can never become a ledger row regardless of model output; hedged text is prefiltered in `shouldRun` so it costs no model spend.
- Finalized meeting transcripts: `handleMeetingTranscriptFinalized` now projects the owner's own diarized segments through the deterministic extractor into the ledger (`src/lifeops/commitments/transcript-hook.ts`); other speakers' promises are ignored.

### 2. Class-level standing guarantees
- `OWNER_DOCUMENTS.guarantee_class` ("always track contract renewals…") installs one idempotent event-triggered ScheduledTask per obligation class (`commitment|renewal|filing|warranty`), keyed `commitment-guarantee:<class>` — no second scheduler, all matching on typed trigger/filter/metadata fields.
- `persistDocumentObligation` (the existing `track_deadline` path, `trackDocumentObligationArtifact`'s production seam) now calls `applyCommitmentClassGuarantees`: a covered artifact gains an idempotent lead-time warn watcher (default 60 days before deadline, `commitment-warn:<doc>:<deadline>:<days>`) and fires the typed `document.obligation.observed` event through the spine's event bridge. Event kind + filter schema registered in the event-kind registry.

### 3. BRIEF / PRIORITIZE consumption
- BRIEF: new `sections.commitments` block built from `buildCommitmentRegretAudit` over open/tracked rows (score + reasons carried through, `include.commitments` opt-out, designed-empty on no-DB hosts).
- PRIORITIZE: new `rank_commitments` subaction / `commitments` subject (similes `WHAT_WILL_I_REGRET`, `REGRET_AUDIT`) ranking the audit's deterministic scores and reasons through the existing single LLM pass.

Respects the open claim on the authenticated regret-audit read route (#19273 successor) — no HTTP surface is added here.

## Tests

Run in the worktree against `origin/develop` (vitest, plugin config):

- `test/commitment-extraction-evaluator.test.ts` — 14 pass (parse guards, verbatim/hedge/confidence rejection, "yeah maybe sometime" creates zero rows through the real processor, shouldRun prefilters).
- `test/commitment-standing-guarantees.test.ts` — 9 pass (install contract, warn-watcher timing + idempotency key, custom lead time, past-lead-time skip, class mismatch, unparseable-deadline throw).
- `test/commitment-transcript-hook.test.ts` — owner-attribution, hedge rejection, typed-empty paths.
- `test/document-action.test.ts` (+4 guarantee_class cases), `test/brief-action.test.ts` (+2 commitments-section cases), `test/prioritize-action.test.ts` (+2 rank_commitments cases) — 63 pass together.
- `bun run typecheck` (tsc `tsconfig.build.json`) clean.

Live-scenario evidence (seeded sent-mail promise surfacing in a live brief, scenario-time 60-day warn fire) remains tracked on the issue's evidence bar; this PR delivers the product path and deterministic contract proof.

## Summary

- add one validated UI boundary for agent-log enqueue and asynchronous job responses
- surface malformed, failed, forbidden, and timed-out requests as explicit error states
- apply one operation-wide abort deadline so a hung enqueue or poll fetch cannot leave permanent loading
- abort superseded/unmounted requests, fence stale completions, and clear prior-agent/prior-request logs before refresh
- sanitize server and infrastructure diagnostics into bounded user-facing failures
- add tablist/tab/tabpanel semantics with roving keyboard navigation and persistent valid IDREF targets

## Contract and safety

- accepts only the authoritative `agent_logs` job type and known status values
- ignores server-supplied polling URLs and constructs the same-origin job endpoint from the validated ID
- keeps backend notices distinct from log content
- does not touch Shared runtime, billing, credentials, providers, staging state, or production

21:T63b,## Parity program P2 (issue #20615) — the data-layer unification centerpiece

New `shared_agent_memories` table **mirroring the core memories row shape** (id/agent/entity/room/world/type/content/embedding+model/created_at) + NOT NULL org/user tenancy with FK cascade and tenant-first indexes — so Shared→Dedicated transfer becomes a shape-preserving row copy (P2.5 transfer proof queued on this merge). CQRS repository with **every WHERE pinning organization_id+user_id+agent_id** (SQL-capture tests), replay-idempotent inserts on stable transport ids (claim replays cannot double-write), exact pgvector cosine search over a bounded recent window (tradeoff documented). `SharedMemoryStore` gated on `SHARED_MEMORY_TABLES_ENABLED=true` (null otherwise — zero behavior change until flagged), wired at the one turn-commit point both engines share: non-streaming after reply lands outside the provider try/catch (storage faults never re-label provider outcomes for settlement); streaming at finish-part before the terminal frame.

Migration 0210 hand-authored per the package's current practice (0206–0209 same; drizzle-kit meta is 128 migrations stale and would emit a forbidden omnibus). Also fixes the stale journal pin in shared-todos-migration.test.ts (red on develop since 0208/0209 appended).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)22:Te90,Fixes #16478

## What

Owner-only admin surface for the account-pool consumer keys shipped in #16461, following the triage guidance on this card: the renderer is **not** wired to the loopback broker API (its bearer secret must never reach the renderer). Instead:

### Server boundary (new)
- `packages/agent/src/api/consumer-key-routes.ts` — `/api/accounts/consumer-keys` (GET list, POST create), `/:id` (PATCH label/enabled/quota), `/:id/rotate` (POST), gated by the canonical `resolveBoundaryRole(req) === "OWNER"` check (403 otherwise, on every verb). Dispatched from `handleAccountsRoutes` ahead of the `:providerId` parse.
- New optional `AgentHostBridge.getAccountPoolConsumerKeyAdmin` facade; `packages/app-core` implements it over the existing `account-pool-consumer-metering` store (single authority — no new store, no digest exposure). The agent still never imports `@elizaos/app-core`; a hostless/standalone agent answers **501**, never a fake-empty list.
- Plaintext keys exist only in create/rotate response bodies; the list surface exposes `keyPrefix` only (asserted by test).

### UI (`packages/ui`)
- Typed, validated `ElizaClient` methods in `client-agent-consumer-keys.ts` (malformed agent replies throw; nothing renders undefined fields).
- `ConsumerKeyPanel` mounted in `AccountManagementPanel` behind `RoleGate minRole="OWNER"` with the standard `OwnerOnlyNotice` fallback. Per the three-state rule: distinct **loading** (skeletons), **error + retry**, **designed-empty**, and list states. Rows show label, key prefix, enabled badge, quota, created/last-used.
- **Create** and **rotate** show the one-time plaintext in a warning banner with Copy and an explicit Dismiss ("shown only once and cannot be recovered") — never persisted, logged, cached, or placed in URLs; dismissing discards the only copy.
- **Rotate** and **disable** require inline confirmation; enable is direct; quota editing validates positive integers client-side and round-trips through PATCH. lucide-react icons only; existing design tokens (no new colors, no blue).
- 30 new `consumerKeys.*` keys added to the source i18n catalog (`check:i18n` green).

## Tests (all green)

- `packages/agent`: `consumer-key-routes.test.ts` — 5 tests: role denial (403) on every verb for non-OWNER, 501 when the host exposes no admin facade, full CRUD happy path incl. one-time plaintext + **no plaintext in the list response**, and 400/404/405 mapping. 🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)23:T9ab,Fixes #14763

## What

Creates `packages/corpus-tools` (the collectors directory was absent on develop; the closed Signal PR #16036 left no reusable infra) with:

- **`src/schema.ts`** — the private corpus interchange `CorpusMessage` schema (zod-validated) and the `2024-07-05` corpus cutoff constant.
- **`src/validator.ts`** — shard/corpus validation and `buildCorpusManifest` producing the canonical `manifest.json`.
- **`src/collectors/x-archive.ts`** — the official X data-archive collector:
  - Reads the archive **ZIP** (fflate, filtered to `data/*.js` during unzip) or an already-extracted directory.
  - Handles all `window.YTD.<name>.partN` files (`tweets.js`, `tweets-part1.js`, …) and strips the script prefix before JSON parsing, failing closed with typed `ElizaError` codes (`X_ARCHIVE_BAD_PREFIX` / `BAD_JSON` / `BAD_TIMESTAMP` / `BAD_ZIP` / `MISSING_FILE`).
  - Drops rows with `created_at < 2024-07-05` (configurable cutoff).
  - Tweets → `CorpusMessage` direction `out`, `threadId` resolved to the reply-chain root within the archive (cycle-safe, falls back to the referenced parent when the chain leaves the archive).
  - DMs → per-conversation messages, direction derived from `senderId` vs owner account id.
  - Likes are **counted in the summary only** — never fabricated into message rows (per triage guidance).
  - Writes deterministic monthly JSONL shards idempotently (byte-identical shards reused → interrupted runs are resumable), plus `manifest.json` and `x-archive-summary.json` with tweet/DM-conversation counts.

Raw archives stay under the gitignored `data/` tree; only the synthetic fixture is committed (`.gitignore` re-includes `fixtures/x-archive/data/`).

## Out of scope (per issue)

API top-up via plugin-x, media bytes, and the owner's real archive pull (needs-human, 24–48 h X latency).

## Tests

Committed synthetic mini-archive fixture (multi-part tweets, two DM conversations, likes). 7 vitest cases: full directory collection + manifest counts, ZIP-input parity, cutoff filtering, DM direction mapping, resume/shard-reuse, reply-chain threadIds, and malformed-archive failure paths (missing file, bad prefix, bad JSON).

bun run --cwd packages/corpus-tools typecheck   # clean
bun run --cwd packages/corpus-tools lint:check  # clean (pinned Biome via run-biome-typescript.mjs)
```

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)24:Td8c,Fixes #18957

## What

New `@elizaos/plugin-companion`: a WebSocket **client** Service connecting to the ESP32-S3 companion device's own server at `ws://<device>:8080/api/companion/device-bridge?token=<PAIRING_TOKEN>` (settings `COMPANION_WS_URL`, `COMPANION_PAIRING_TOKEN` — both required, fail-fast typed errors when absent). Opt-in only: no `autoEnable`, registered via `UNBUNDLED_OPTIONAL_PLUGINS` with `skipOnMobile`, never baked into the mobile APK.

### Acceptance criteria mapping

- **UC1** `SET_COMPANION_MOOD` sends `SET_MOOD`, awaits the `commandResult` with matching `correlationId`, returns the device-confirmed mood; device `ok:false` (e.g. mood `angry`) is a typed `COMPANION_COMMAND_REJECTED` failure surfaced as a failed `ActionResult`.
- **UC2** `GET_COMPANION_STATUS` returns `deviceId`, mood, connected, firmware, capabilities; a disconnected device fails closed (`COMPANION_NOT_CONNECTED`), never empty-healthy.
- **UC3** Device `event touch` is stored on the service and surfaced by the `companionDevice` provider (`companionLastEvent=touch`); `mood_changed` updates the tracked mood.
- **UC4** Token appended as `?token=`; a rejected socket never becomes ready and commands fail closed. No host frame (not even ping) leaves before the handshake — verified by asserting the mock device received zero frames.
- **UC5** `welcome` → `register` gating; identity (`deviceId`, `firmware`, `capabilities.platform`) recorded from `register`; a register without `deviceId` is a reported bad frame and NOT connected.
- **UC6** Host `ping` → device `pong` keepalive; a missed pong marks the socket stale, terminates it, rejects pending commands (`COMPANION_NOT_CONNECTED`), and schedules a bounded reconnect. A socket-generation counter prevents stale timers/frames from resurrecting state.
- **UC7** Malformed JSON, unknown frame types, and uncorrelated `commandResult`s are reported via `runtime.reportError` and ignored; the service keeps working (verified: a subsequent `SET_MOOD` succeeds).

`src/protocol.ts` is the single inbound trust boundary — every frame is structurally validated once into a typed union; downstream code never touches raw JSON. Retained catches carry `error-policy:J<N>` classifications.

## Tests

`bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-companion test` — **16/16 passed**: a deterministic mock device built on a real in-process `ws` `WebSocketServer` (no `vi.mock`, no hardware, no egress) covering configuration fail-fast, handshake/identity, bad token, register-without-deviceId, correlation matching incl. an interleaved uncorrelated result, invalid mood (service + action), status + disconnect fail-closed, missing-mood parameter, touch/mood_changed events + provider, keepalive healthy/stale paths, and malformed-frame resilience.

Also green: plugin `typecheck` + `lint:check`; agent registration drift suites `optional-plugins.test.ts` + `core-static-plugin-registrations.test.ts` (19/19) and `core-plugins-profile-metadata.test.ts` (12/12); `ensure-plugin-test-conventions:check`; `check:agents-claude` (153 pairs).

## Evidence caveat

Real ESP32 hardware output and a live-model action trajectory are not included — no companion device is attached to this runner. Mock-device coverage is presented as exactly that; hardware runs remain evidence-only per the issue ("Tests use a mock WebSocket device; hardware is evidence-only, not a CI gate").

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)25:Te01,## What

Finishes the two remaining gaps of #17024 and absorbs the STOP-envelope repair from #20366.

### Bounded planner dialogue

`createV5MessageContextObject` now includes at most the four newest ordinary assistant replies so continuation turns can resolve their referent. Tool-derived replies remain structurally excluded through action callback history or real tool action names, and the planner prompt marks all prior facts as stale.

### Deterministic continuation resolution

The direct-action heuristics now distinguish conservative, whole-message continuation directives from approvals. They resolve only the nearest prior user request; approval additionally requires the latest visible agent reply to remain pending. Requester identity is bound, so another participant cannot continue someone else's request. Topic switches remain independent requests.

### STOP remains a non-tool envelope

The canonical `STOP` action name is centralized and treated as planner-terminal metadata, not evidence of a completed tool-derived reply. An approval following a pending reply marked `STOP` can therefore continue the request, while real tool-derived results still cannot replay it.

### Live trajectory proof

The key-gated integration test uses the real runtime, PGlite persistence, plugin-openai through Cerebras, and persisted prior user/assistant turns. It proves three paths:

1. `finish my request` executes the pending SHELL action exactly once.
2. `that is good` after a pending `STOP` reply executes SHELL exactly once.
3. A Tokyo weather topic switch executes WEB_SEARCH exactly once and never replays SHELL.

## Residual risk

- Planner prompt bytes change for tool turns that have recent ordinary assistant replies; the four-turn bound limits token and cache impact.
- Continuation phrases are intentionally conservative. Unrecognized phrasings fall through to normal planner behavior instead of risking false-positive replay.

26:T5871,# Fixes

Relates to https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/18045 (kept open: the deployed staging create → first-send proof remains tracked by #18045; this PR delivers the independently mergeable client slice) — the **client slice** completing the first-shared-agent-turn warming contract. The server/API slice (stable machine codes, `Retry-After: 1`, durable `clientMessageId` claim/replay, narrowed e2e classifier) merged in #19078; this PR covers the client-side items that comment left open: bounded warming absorption in the send path and the terminal `insufficient_credits` mapping.

Shipped here, per the issue's "Expected behavior":

- **The original send stays pending while the client absorbs only the structured warming responses.** The absorption lives at the request choke point (`ElizaClient.rawRequest`), so every browser consumer of the shared chat surfaces — messages REST, messages/stream, and the recovery/replay paths that route through it — gets the same behavior: a `503` whose body carries `code: agent_cache_warming` or `code: shared_runtime_cache_warming` waits the advertised `Retry-After` (clamped 250 ms–5 s) and re-issues the **byte-identical request**, so the same `clientMessageId` rides every retry and lands in #19078's idempotent claim/replay boundary. Budget: 4 re-issues **and** a ~5 s total-elapsed deadline across all warming waits (`WARMING_TOTAL_BUDGET_MS`) — an oversized `Retry-After` gets its wait clamped to the remaining budget instead of stretching to attempts × max-delay — matching the issue's "four attempts / roughly five seconds". The first warming wait also surfaces the existing `waking` turn status instead of stalled dots.

**Review response (changes-requested, 2026-08-13):** `rawRequest` is now ONE bounded classification loop — every response, first attempt or any retry, re-enters the same 401/202/warming classifier, so the states compose in any order: a 202 that follows an absorbed warming 503 runs the full resume contract (no placeholder "success"), a 401 mid-sequence still gets its one refresh, and the refreshed token is written back so every later re-issue (warming or resume) carries it. The 402 classification `useChatSend` consumes moved out of `/join` into a domain-neutral `packages/ui/src/api/credit-gate-error.ts`; `/join` keeps only the welcome-bonus interpretation as a wrapper over that canonical classifier.
- **The first non-warming response is surfaced immediately** — the loop exits on the first response that is not a named warming 503, success or failure.
- **A generic/unstructured 503 is not retried** (`inference_unavailable`, cache outage, code-less 503s stay real failures), **and a 402 is never retried**.
- **A terminal `code: insufficient_credits` JSON 402 now renders the existing out-of-credits turn** (banner + "Add credits" CTA, no Retry chip) instead of falling through `useChatSend` as a generic `provider_issue` Retry turn that re-hits the empty balance. The classifier is the same fail-closed 402 walk the /join surface uses (`describeJoinCreditGateError`), and the turn shows the server's own message.
- **E2E covering the warm-up never becoming user-visible failures:** a new real-browser e2e (`test:warming-absorption-e2e`) mounts the production `useChatSend` → `streamChatEndpoint` → `rawRequest` pipeline + ChatOverlay with the server simulated at the transport boundary, replays the issue's exact staging sequence (`agent_cache_warming` 503 → `shared_runtime_cache_warming` 503 → streamed reply), and asserts the send stays pending with no Retry chip and no error notice until the reply lands — then asserts the 402 renders terminally.

Reachability: this is the production first-shared-agent-chat path — the user-visible new-user QA flow the issue reproduces on staging. The absorption sits in the shared browser client, so the deployed runtime's named warming codes from #19078 are consumed by exactly this code.

Definition of Done: full standard in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](../CONTRIBUTING.md).

# Risks

**Low.** The warming loop triggers only on a `503` whose parsed body carries one of the two named codes — a shape no other server response uses — and is bounded (4 re-issues), abort-aware (caller `AbortSignal` short-circuits both the wait and the re-issue), and skipped entirely for `allowNonOk` probes, so readiness/status polls keep their raw responses. Retrying is idempotent by server contract: both barriers reject **before** admission (#19078), and the re-issued body is byte-identical, so the durable `clientMessageId` claim de-dupes any request that did land. The 402 mapping only fires on the canonical `status === 402 && code === insufficient_credits` shape (fail-closed classifier); a code-less 402 keeps today's generic path (covered by a test).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

First shared-agent chat: instead of surfacing the two expected cache-warming 503s as user-visible failures before the first reply, the client absorbs them inside the original pending send and shows the first real response. A drained-balance 402 becomes the designed out-of-credits turn instead of a retry loop that cannot succeed.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Background

- Uses one parsed same-origin return destination sanitizer across query, persisted, callback, and waiting-tab paths.
- Preserves explicit resend/back actions for expired, consumed, invalid, and incomplete callbacks.

# Sync with develop

# Risks

The change is isolated to core model routing and evaluator input budgeting. It adds an internal per-attempt preparation hook so the budget is derived from the provider registration that the runtime actually selected, including owner overrides and same-model-type failovers. Unknown model metadata retains the conservative 128k fallback and is identified as unresolved in terminal diagnostics.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Resolves and applies the evaluator context budget for every actual provider attempt instead of pre-compacting all input to the smallest reachable fallback.
- Preserves large-context input byte-for-byte until a smaller selected attempt requires compaction.
- Recomputes cache metadata and keeps evaluator thinking disabled after every rerender.
- Guards structured tool-call parameters and rejects a still-over-budget attempt before its provider handler runs.
- Records a typed `EVALUATOR_INPUT_OVER_BUDGET` evaluation stage without falsely recording a provider failure.
- Keeps custom sub-10k context windows usable by capping the output reserve relative to the model window.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

No documentation changes are needed; this is an internal runtime contract fix.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

Start with `packages/core/src/runtime/evaluator.ts`, the per-attempt hook in `packages/core/src/runtime.ts`, and `packages/core/src/runtime/__tests__/evaluator-input-budget.test.ts`.

# Sync with develop

- [ ] `bun run verify` not run post-sync in this isolated worktree — exact blocker documented in **Known gaps / failures**.

# Risks

Low. The change tightens two guards on an already-conservative deterministic resolver: continuation resolution now returns `null` in cases where it previously returned another participant's text or a completed request. The failure mode of the fix is a missed continuation (agent asks again) instead of a wrong side-effecting action inference. `isToolDerivedAssistantContent` moved from `message.ts` into `direct-action-heuristics.ts` verbatim, so the planner prior-dialogue exclusion behavior is unchanged (covered by the existing stage-1 test).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Fixes the two independently breakable defects the #20227 review reproduced at head `88f8235a8`:

1. **Cross-participant continuation resolution.** `resolveExplicitContinuationRequestText` excluded only the agent from the candidate loop, and `RECENT_MESSAGES` is room-scoped, so User B saying `go ahead` could resolve to User A's nearest request (the review's adversarial case returned `"delete my deployment"` instead of `null`). The resolver now takes the current requester's `entityId` (threaded from `message.entityId` at the single production call site) and only ever resolves that entity's own prior request. It also refuses to resolve when the requester is the agent itself.

2. **Completed action replayed as pending.** The approval gate only consulted `actionCallbackHistory`, while the message path already treats `content.actions` (anything beyond the reply/none/ignore envelope) as a structural tool-derived marker. A completed `{ text: "Done.", actions: ["DELETE_DEPLOYMENT"] }` reply fit the 160-char "looks pending" fallback and replayed the finished request (the review's case returned `"delete the stale deployment"` instead of `null`). The shared predicate `isToolDerivedAssistantContent` now lives in `direct-action-heuristics.ts`, is folded into `isContinuationDialogueArtifact` (covering both the approval gate and candidate loop), and `message.ts` imports it instead of keeping a private copy — one predicate, used consistently.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking; `resolveExplicitContinuationRequestText` is module-internal to `@elizaos/core` — not exported from any package entrypoint — so the added required parameter has exactly one production caller, updated in the same commit).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

- `packages/core/src/services/message/direct-action-heuristics.ts` — the resolver: requester binding, shared `isToolDerivedAssistantContent`, artifact check.
- `packages/core/src/services/message/direct-action-heuristics.test.ts` — three new unit regressions (cross-participant → `null`, requester-bound resolution with both participants asking, completed-`content.actions` → `null` with `actions: ["REPLY"]` control).
- `packages/core/src/__tests__/message-runtime-stage1.test.ts` — two new production-path regressions through `runV5MessageRuntimeStage1` (real stage-1 runtime, mocked model responses): another participant's pending request is not promoted, and a completed `content.actions` result is not replayed — in both, the shell handler is never invoked and the turn stays a simple reply.

## What changed

- Adds one canonical `PublicWebGrounding` contract in core with strict provider/provenance validation, bounded UTF-8 query/result/aggregate encoding, `observedAt`, `truncated`, and collision-safe structured `untrusted_public_web_search_result` payloads.
- Keeps assistant prose separate from grounding. Both direct AI SDK history and Shared `AgentRuntime` history project web results as native assistant tool-call/tool-result content under a system invariant that persisted web content is untrusted data, never instructions, authorization, or tool requests.
- Selects grounding only from trusted stored query/assistant-prose overlap, plus the immediate prior turn for a deictic follow-up. Raw result text cannot term-stuff itself into model context.
- Preserves validated grounding when stale Durable Object/Postgres copies of the same message are merged. Conflicts resolve deterministically by `observedAt`, then canonical encoding.
- Keeps REST and SSE on the same Shared history/finalization path.
- Extends the authenticated Shared-to-Dedicated cutover import contract with bounded grounding metadata. The Agent import validates assistant-only grounding, persists it as memory metadata, and core projects it at the same untrusted tool-result tier without appending it to visible transcript text.
- Coordinates with #20186 at the shared chat boundary without importing that PR wholesale.

2c:Ta52,Part of #20313

## What this phase does

This is the rollback-safe foundation for sender-scoped Personal Shared account projections:

- adds one Durable Object per connector sender for Shared user/org resolution
- caches only stable Shared account identifiers; Dedicated lifecycle/config remains canonical
- serializes concurrent cold hydration and persists before serving the in-memory projection
- invalidates on Telegram/Discord identity mutation, Steward convergence, and Shared-to-Dedicated cutover
- adds exact resolution telemetry without logging message or credential content
- deploys the binding and all invalidation writers with PERSONAL_DELIVERY_PROJECTION_READ_ENABLED=false

Reads deliberately remain on the canonical database path in this PR. A later reviewed activation PR may set the gate to the exact string true only after this foundation is the deployed rollback baseline. That prevents a binding-absent rollback from silently missing invalidations and later resurrecting stale Durable Object state.

2d:Ta84,Fixes #16290

## Problem

The root `postinstall` ran `packages/scripts/sync-artifacts.mjs`, which downloaded a ~1 GB stale snapshot from `elizaOS/eliza-archive`, extracted it **over the repository root** (rewriting tracked files with different bytes after a clean checkout), and **exited 0** after exhausted downloads, sha256 mismatches, or extraction failures — a checkout-wide source-integrity hazard hidden inside every `bun install`.

## Change

- **Removed** the `sync-artifacts.mjs` postinstall invocation and the `sync:artifacts` root script; deleted the script, `artifacts-manifest.json`, and its test. `install:light` becomes a plain `bun install` alias (there is no artifact sync left to skip).
- **Added** `packages/scripts/fetch-archive-artifacts.mjs` — the explicit opt-in path for consumers that genuinely need a legacy fixture/media/binary. It verifies the pinned sha256, fails **closed** (non-zero exit + actionable message) on download/digest/extraction failure, supports `--include <prefix>` scoping, and **refuses to extract anywhere inside the checkout**, so archive content can never silently rewrite tracked files again.
- **Added** contract test `packages/scripts/__tests__/no-global-archive-hook.test.ts`: no root lifecycle script (`preinstall`/`install`/`postinstall`/`prepare`/`prepublish`) may reference an archive sync/fetch, `sync:artifacts` must stay gone, and the retired machinery must stay deleted.
- **Cleaned** dead references: `ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC` env in `quality.yml`, `sync-artifacts.mjs` path filters in `build-agent-image.yml`/`cloud-cf-deploy.yml`, the `.eliza-artifacts-version` ignore, and prose comments in `test-cloud-run*.mjs` / `cloud-tests.yml`. Root `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` updated in lockstep.

The gitignored artifact paths stay ignored: those files remain local-only, now provisioned deliberately per consumer instead of by a silent global hook.

## Verification

- `bun install` in a fresh worktree of this branch completes with **zero contact with elizaOS/eliza-archive** and creates no `.eliza-artifacts-version` marker.
- `bun test packages/scripts/__tests__/no-global-archive-hook.test.ts packages/scripts/__tests__/cloud-release-dependency-trigger-workflow.test.ts` → 8 pass, 0 fail (the release-dependency closure test re-validates the postinstall/workflow path parity after the removal).
- Fetch-script failure paths exercised for real: missing `--dest` → exit 1 with guidance; `--dest` inside the checkout → refused, exit 1; `--help` → usage, exit 0.
- `bun run check:agents-claude` → 153 pairs byte-identical; `biome check` clean on new files.

# Sync with develop

# Risks

Security-positive and backward-compatible for ready/legacy documents. Incomplete ingestions become invisible on list/search until a later retry deletes the exact failed snapshot. OWNER snapshot CAS/delete still reaches those rows for cleanup. SQL `countMemories` now honors the documented metadata filter; callers that previously relied on the silent drop would have been counting the wrong set.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

#19941 added best-effort compensation after fragment embedding failed, but the parent row used a deterministic content ID, `createMemory` reports no insert-vs-conflict signal, and cleanup scanned a global 10k fragment page. A concurrent loser could delete the winner, leftover fragments made retries look healthy, and more than 10,000 fragments were not guaranteed to disappear.

This repair claims each attempt with `ingestionAttemptId` + `ingestionState`, verifies ownership after insert, writes fragments only for the winner, marks `failed` before `deleteDocumentWithSnapshot`, and hides pending/failed rows from authorized reads. Fragment counts use metadata-filtered `countMemories` instead of a capped global scan.

## What kind of change is this?

Data-integrity and authorization-adjacent hardening of document ingestion.

# Risks

Medium operational risk, fail-closed. Production Cloud releases will stop before the protected production environment and before provider mutation unless the exact candidate Git tree has an unexpired successful staging certification. This is intentionally stricter than the current workflow. Staging releases are unchanged except for a post-success, read-only checkout and immutable artifact upload. Certification expires after 14 days and cannot be bypassed by `force`.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Successful `push`/`develop` Cloud releases now emit a 14-day immutable certificate only after the reusable release and all of its migration, Worker, Pages, routing, and postdeploy checks succeed. The certificate binds repository, workflow bytes, run id/attempt, staging commit, exact root Git tree, event/ref/environment, artifact name, and validity window.

Production preflight checks out the exact requested `main` SHA, computes `HEAD^{tree}`, resolves the newest unexpired artifact with the deterministic tree-bound name, verifies the originating GitHub run and immutable artifact metadata, downloads by exact artifact id with digest checking, and validates the payload. Only then can the protected `production` approval job or reusable mutation workflow become reachable.

Different `develop` and `main` commit SHAs are accepted only when their complete Git trees are byte-identical. Missing, expired, renamed, digestless, malformed, fork-owned, failed-run, wrong-event/branch/workflow/environment/tree, or changed-workflow evidence is rejected. `force` has no path around the gate.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and release-safety hardening.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated the Cloud workflow inventory with certificate issuance, production validation, TTL, tree identity, and fail-closed behavior.

# Risks

Medium. This changes timing authority for one-off Shared reminders. The parser must not mistake body text for timing, persist a negated/cancelled request, truncate a compound duration, cross into reminder content while reading revisions, or let planner parameters override an explicit authenticated user delay. The implementation therefore uses bounded command structures, ordered negation/cancellation parsing, exact duration composition, and fails closed before persistence on ambiguity.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Reconciles a supported explicit relative delay from the authenticated current user message before constructing the Shared reminder trigger.
- Makes literal `in 1 minute` persist exactly `action-now + 60,000ms` even if the planner emits `inMinutes: 2`.
- Supports compound and compact seconds/minutes/hours, `and a half`, and bounded later revisions.
- Rejects negation, later cancellation, cancellation synonyms, malformed/overflow values, multiple directives, and alternatives before any scheduler write.
- Keeps reminder body phrases such as `cancel that meeting` valid while treating terminal `cancel that` as a no-write cancellation.
- Accepts bounded `actually` / `please` modifiers in either order without crossing into the reminder body.
- Leaves the canonical ScheduledTask runner, delivery contract, and idempotency key unchanged.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix.

# Documentation changes needed?

No. The public reminder parameters and canonical scheduling architecture are unchanged.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)34:Te59,## Summary

On cloud-only desktop builds the home pill now rests in a **`needs-auth`** phase while there is no Cloud session: a labeled **"Sign in to Eliza"** chip that launches the interactive Cloud login instead of opening the overlay or the mic. Previously the pill was fully interactive (open overlay, hold-to-talk, fn-hold PTT, hands-free, realtime voice) even when signed out, which made no sense — every path just 401'd.

Follow-up to the fn-hold PTT work in #20483.

## State machine

`needs-auth` sits **above** the live waterfall so a signed-out pill can never grow into the waveform chip:

```
needs-auth > listening > processing > responding > summoned > idle/booting
```

- Gate derivation is pure (`shell-auth-gate.ts`): `cloudOnly` branding + the shared `/api/auth/me` snapshot (subscribe-only — no extra network traffic).
- `loading` / `server_unavailable` hold the closed launcher on `booting` so a slow probe never flashes the sign-in chip.
- The `cloudOnly` guard is the native-owner-key trap: a local desktop owner token must not look like a Cloud session, so non-cloud-only builds are byte-identical (gate always `{ gated: false }`).

## Gated paths (controller)

- `startCapture` / `startRecording` / `toggleRecording` / `toggleHandsFree` / `startRealtimeVoice` refuse while gated; user-initiated ones call `requestSignIn()` instead.
- `open()` launches sign-in instead of summoning the overlay.
- Sign-out mid-session cancels in-flight capture, closes the overlay, and stops realtime voice.
- `requestSignIn` claims the login popup **synchronously on the user gesture** (`claimCloudLoginWindow`) so the popup is never blocked into same-tab (#17064 contract; callsite added to the sanctioned list in `cloud-login-callsite-contract.test.ts`).
- Desktop shell fn-hold PTT + global toggle hotkeys route to `requestSignIn` while gated.

## Native window (electrobun)

- The transparent bottom-bar window gains an intermediate **240×56 chip frame** (`resolveBottomBarFrameSize`) between the 96×56 rest pill and the 600×820 expanded overlay, and now remembers its size across work-area re-anchors.
- `desktopSetBottomBarExpanded` RPC gains an optional `chip` flag.
- Follower/snapshot sync accepts `needs-auth` (and the previously missing `processing`) as shell phases.

## Verification

- **Unit:** 33 new tests (`shell-auth-gate.test.ts`, `useShellAuthGate.test.tsx`) + updated `HomePill` / `useShellController` / sync suites. Full `packages/ui` lane: **10,647 passed**. Electrobun lane: **538 passed**.
- **Gates:** typecheck + lint clean across all 356 workspace tasks; every `bun run verify` step passes except the pre-existing `ensure-plugin-test-conventions` failure (orphaned llama.cpp submodule webui tests) which also fails on clean `develop` — unrelated.
- **Visual:** `bun run --cwd packages/app audit:app` — 219/219 passed, 0 broken / 0 needs-work. Storybook gains `NeedsAuth` / `NeedsAuthSigningIn` stories (covered by the story gate).
- **Live desktop (macOS, real Electrobun app, signed out via cleared WebKit store, `ELIZA_DESKTOP_CLOUD_ONLY=1`):**
  - `/api/auth/me` → real 401 from `api.eliza.app`; native window resized to **240×56** and the accessibility tree reads `toggle button "Sign in to Eliza"` (was `"Open Eliza"`).
  - Clicking the chip launched the Cloud sign-in flow; after the session was adopted the gate cleared, the window returned to **96×56**, and the pill read `"Open Eliza"` and opened the overlay normally.
  - Signed-in cloud-only boot (existing session) never flashes the chip: probe resolves `authenticated` and the pill rests idle.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)35:Tfbc,Fixes #15973

## Problem

The context-aware PII pseudonym stage merged in #15841 was unreachable in production. On develop:
- no production package registers a `ModelType.PII_SCRUB` handler (every registration lives under tests), so `scrubWithEscalation` fail-closes on any residue in a live run;
- nothing in the shipped scrub workflow calls `assembleContextPack()` or loads the encrypted corpus pseudonym map, so even with a handler the verdicts would be context-free and pseudonyms inconsistent across items and restarts.

## Change

**Production `PII_SCRUB` model route** (`plugins/plugin-local-inference`):
- `src/pii/scrub-handler.ts` — constrained JSON-judgment prompt builder + fail-closed completion parser. Every escalated candidate must receive a verdict (a dropped candidate throws, never "clean"); a `pii` verdict must carry a usable replacement; a supplied cluster assignment's surrogate is used verbatim regardless of what the model emitted, so corpus-wide pseudonym consistency is structural, not model-trusted.
- `src/provider.ts` — `createPiiScrubHandler()` registered in `createLocalInferenceModelHandlers()` (and therefore on the static plugin object), running on the resident local backend through the inference priority gate at `background` priority. The prompt is deliberately not budget-clamped: judging a truncated fragment would corrupt verdicts, so an over-budget scrub fails and the rails retry.

**Stage wiring in the single existing scrub workflow** (`packages/core/src/services/pii-scrub.ts`, no second scheduler/mechanism):
- Requests arriving without a pre-assembled pack now run the #15841 stage inside the existing drain: load the encrypted corpus map (`EncryptedCachePseudonymMapStore`), assemble the context pack + per-chunk assignment slice (`assembleContextPack`, entity resolution via the knowledge-graph service when registered), and hand both to the seam.
- After a successful scrub the grown map is persisted **before** the done-marker, so a marked-done item always has its assignments durably in the encrypted artifact; nothing persists on the failure path.
- A tampered/wrong-key map artifact throws (fail-closed) and routes through the existing retry/quarantine path — never a context-free scrub that would mint inconsistent pseudonyms.
- Pre-assembled requests (`buildScrubRequestDraft` producers) pass through untouched.

## Tests (all passing locally)

`packages/core` — `bunx vitest run src/services/pii-scrub.test.ts src/services/pii-scrub-context-stage.test.ts` → **13 passed**
- new suite drives the service's real request entry + drain with the REAL encrypted store/map/seam (only the model judge is scripted):
  - stage assembly: model call carries the assembled `contextPack` + `pseudonymAssignments`; map persisted as `v2:` ciphertext (never plaintext, never the alias); marker written after the map save
  - **restart persistence**: a fresh service over the same durable cache reuses the SAME surrogate for the same entity in different content
  - pre-assembled passthrough: stage skipped, no map artifact touched
  - tampered artifact: zero model calls, no done-marker, `PII_SCRUB_FAILED` + `reportError` after retries
- the pre-existing rails suite still passes unchanged (tier-0 short-circuit, idempotency, crash/retry fail-closed).

`plugins/plugin-local-inference` — `bunx vitest run src/pii/scrub-handler.test.ts` → **11 passed** (prompt content, parser fail-closed contracts, assignment-surrogate override, handler exercised through the production model map, backend-absent fail-closed).

Typecheck: `packages/core` tsc clean; `plugins/plugin-local-inference` tsc clean. Biome clean on all touched files.

Per the issue's narrowed scope, the exhaustive adversarial-corpus acceptance list is follow-up evidence; this PR makes the stage reachable by a normal scrub run with a live local model and proves map application + persistence across restart through the production entry point.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)36:T1238,## Summary

Wispr Flow parity for the global voice gesture (#20483): **hold the fn (Globe) key from any app to talk to Eliza** — down opens the mic (audible ping + the pill grows into the listening chip), release sends, and a chorded release (fn+arrow etc.) cancels without sending. No window opens and no focus is stolen; the pill alone shows the state.

Until now the OS-wide hotkey was a *toggle* (Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+Space) because Electrobun's `GlobalShortcut` is trigger-only — no key-up ever reaches the app, and fn isn't a registrable accelerator at all. This PR adds the missing native seam.

Also included by product decision: the red hot-mic ring on the listening chip is removed — the dark chip + live waveform bars alone carry "mic is hot"; the red outline read as an error border.

## How Wispr does it (research)

Dissected the installed Wispr Flow app (Electron, v1.6.529): it ships a separate signed Swift helper (`swift-helper-app-dist/…`, `LSUIElement`) spawned over a pipe-fd JSON IPC; the helper links `CGEventTapCreate/Enable`, watches `flagsChanged` for fn down/up, and runs a `TapWatchdog` that re-enables the tap when macOS disables it.

We don't need a helper app or an Electrobun fork: the repo already has a signed in-process native seam — the `bun:ffi` dylib (`window-effects.mm`). The fn monitor lives there, with the same watchdog semantics Wispr ships.

## Implementation

- **`native/macos/window-effects.mm`** — listen-only `CGEventTap` on `flagsChanged` (keycode 63 / `kCGEventFlagMaskSecondaryFn`) on a dedicated CFRunLoop thread; transitions land in a lock-free SPSC ring buffer; the tap self-re-enables on `kCGEventTapDisabledBy*`; exposes start/stop/poll/health/keystate plus an `AppleFnUsageType` reader (the system "Press 🌐 key to…" action collides with a bare fn tap and a listen-only tap can't swallow it, so the renderer warns when it isn't "Do Nothing").
- **`src/native/desktop.ts`** — 16 ms drain poller + 5 s health watchdog that stop/starts the tap; dedupe + overflow resync against the physical key state (a stuck "held" chip can't outlive the actual hold); teardown pushes a cancelled release; wired into `dispose()`.
- **RPC** — `desktopStartFnHoldMonitor` / `desktopStopFnHoldMonitor` requests and the `desktopFnHoldChanged {held, cancelled}` push.
- **Renderer** — new `PUSH_TO_TALK_HOLD_EVENT` drives the *same* pill hold path as press-and-hold (`startRecording("ptt")` / `stopRecording()` / `cancelRecording()` + capture cues). `permission-missing` (no Accessibility trust yet) and non-mac/sandboxed builds degrade silently to the existing toggle hotkey.

## Evidence

- **Live native proof:** synthesized fn hold (CGEvent `flagsChanged`, keycode 63, 1.2 s) against the built dylib — monitor reports `down` at 1105 ms and `up` at 2291 ms, matching the hold window; `healthy: true`.
- **Live packaged proof:** built, signed, and launched the packaged `Eliza-dev.app` from a fully wiped state (WebKit storage/session tokens/preferences deleted); holding fn grows the pill into the dark listening chip with live waveform bars and releases back to the handle. User-confirmed working in the fresh cloud-only consumer build.
- **Unit tests:** new `desktop-fn-hold.test.ts` (9 tests) covers start-status + usage-type reporting, permission-missing (no poller), hold→send, chord-cancel, dedupe/unpaired-release, overflow resync, watchdog restart, and teardown-mid-hold. Electrobun suite 74 files / 537 tests green; `HomePill` suite updated for the ring removal (24 green); shell suite 1059 green; typecheck green in all four touched packages.
- Pre-existing failures on develop (character-presets templates, todo-cutover ENOTDIR, llama.cpp orphaned-test conventions, two app script tests) reproduce on a clean develop checkout and are unrelated.

## Notes for reviewers

- The listen-only tap needs **Accessibility** (or Input Monitoring) trust; the start result distinguishes `permission-missing` so the renderer never mistakes a denied tap for a hard failure. Surfacing a first-class permission prompt card in onboarding is deliberate follow-up scope.
- Store (MAS) builds are sandboxed — event taps can't work there; those builds keep the toggle hotkey (the RPC reports `unavailable`).
- fn+key chords are deliberately ignored: `kCGEventKeyDown` during a hold marks the release `cancelled`, so typing fn+arrow never sends a phantom voice message.

Closes nothing on its own; tracked under #20483 (gap: "global hold-hotkey push-to-talk", previously blocked on the #12184 fork family — this lands it without fork changes).

37:Tf81,## Summary

Bounded slice of #14872 (Relates to #14872 — the issue-wide live-model scenario evidence and optimization-reward plumbing remain open; see scope notes below).

This lands the pieces of the brief feedback loop that were still missing on develop after #15095 (ledger schema + editorial contract) and #18584 (summary loading during BRIEF), designed around the delivery-boundary and targeted-write findings that blocked #19252:

1. **Truthful rendered impressions at the delivery boundary** — the BRIEF action persists one `rendered` row per surfaced (non-omitted) editorial item only after the callback delivery of the composed brief resolved (`plugins/plugin-personal-assistant/src/actions/brief.ts`). No callback ⇒ nothing was shown ⇒ no rows; a rejected callback propagates before the write. Rows carry `briefingKind`/`period`/`decision` metadata so evening/weekly results are attributable and never masquerade as morning visibility. Ledger write failure degrades observably via `runtime.reportError` (error-policy J7), never retracting a delivered brief.
2. **Owner-facing `recalibrate` / `reset_recalibration` verbs** — new BRIEF subactions. `recalibrate` summarizes the ledger bounded to rows existing at the command instant (`untilIso = commandAt`, per the temporal-ordering finding on #19252), demotes classes the owner has been shown ≥5 times and never acted on — or exactly one explicitly named class — and reports the decision with counts and the reversal instruction. `reset_recalibration` restores classes. Candidate filtering happens before any write, so a targeted command can never write a marker for an unrelated class (the targeted-recalibration regression from the #19252 review is covered directly).
3. **Explicit, reversible markers with precedence** — summaries now track `lastDemotedAt`/`lastKeptAt`; an explicit `demoted` marker demotes regardless of counts, a newer (or same-instant) `kept` marker restores regardless of counts, and only marker-free classes fall back to the automatic ignore rule. No synthetic `ignored` rows are fabricated (the check-then-insert expiry race from #19252 has no analogue here — every write is owner-command-driven).
4. **Editorial contract in the brief prompt** — `BRIEF_NARRATIVE_INSTRUCTIONS` now instructs the compose model to open with the `lead` item, cap at `editorial.maxItems`, give `demote` items at most a passing mention, never resurface `omit` items, and justify exactly one cut when `pushback` is set.

- Inferred production reply/open/keep capture and optimization-reward plumbing into the `morning_brief` dataset.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Both SIWE cloud-onboarding device lanes stopped after home-launcher visibility and durable-state assertions and never invoked the shared liveness contract (#14359), so a provisioned agent that answered nothing — or answered with the stub fixture — still produced a green lane. This wires both lanes to the contract and removes the opt-out that made it fail open.

- **Android** (`test/android/cloud-onboarding.android.spec.ts`): both modes end with one real chat turn through `assertOnboardingLiveness`, using a run-unique challenge prompt so a cached reply cannot satisfy it, and attach a `reply-liveness.jpg` still.
- **iOS in-app verifier** (`src/main.tsx`): the cloud path always drives `driveIosLivenessChatTurn` and reports `livenessReply`. Previously the request parser defaulted `liveness` to `false`, so the cloud lane never talked to the agent at all.
- **iOS harness** (`scripts/ios-cloud-onboarding-smoke.mjs`): sends the run-unique challenge and enforces the reply with the shared `assertLiveReply`.

If the agent never replies, `driveIosLivenessChatTurn` throws and the J1 smoke boundary writes `phase: "failed"`; the lane fails loudly rather than reporting a healthy result.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix — closes a fail-open path in two device lanes.

# Maintainer reduction (2026-08-13)

The submitted revision carried two new test files, `android-cloud-onboarding-liveness-wiring.test.ts` and `ios-cloud-onboarding-liveness-wiring.test.ts` (258 of the 363 added lines). They read `main.tsx`, `ios-cloud-onboarding-smoke.mjs`, and the Android spec as **text** and asserted regex matches against them — `expect(harnessSource).toMatch(/cryptoRandomHex/)`, `expect(specSource).not.toContain("LIVENESS_ENABLED")`, and so on. Those assertions go red on an innocuous rename and stay green when the wiring they describe is behaviourally broken, so they are not evidence of anything. Their only genuinely behavioural coverage — `assertLiveReply` / `isLiveReply` across the real, stub, empty, non-string and labelled cases — already exists on develop in `packages/app/test/liveness-contract.test.ts`. Both files are removed.

Also removed: the vestigial `liveness` field on the cloud smoke request. Nothing read it after this change — the verifier drives the turn unconditionally — so a caller sending `liveness: false` would have been silently ignored, which is the same quiet opt-out this PR set out to delete. The request now carries only `mode` and `livenessPrompt`, and the harness reuses `assertLiveReply` instead of hand-rolling a second copy of the empty/stub-marker rule.

Net: **3 files, +89 / −8**, down from 5 files, +363 / −6.

3a:T12c2,## Summary

Makes the iOS Screen Time surface truthful and build-verifiable instead of advertising host-readable activity data that Apple's privacy-sandboxed Device Activity report extension does not expose to the Capacitor host.

- Render category totals inside the `DeviceActivityReportExtension`, traversing Apple's real multi-segment data shape.
- Centralize the host capability result so the TypeScript bridge reports only what the host can actually read.
- Add localized empty/report copy and deterministic aggregation coverage.
- Validate the app, monitor extension, and report extension entitlements, signing settings, products, and embedding.
- Keep Android's legitimate host-readable coarse-summary contract intact.

This is the current-develop, fully validated superset of the older six-path draft #20044 and supersedes it without overwriting its history.

Closes #20007.

## Root cause

The previous implementation conflated two authorities: authorization for Apple's Family Controls framework and availability of Screen Time data to the host process. On iOS, usage details remain inside the privacy-sandboxed Device Activity report extension. The host bridge therefore advertised data it could not actually return, while the report extension used a static placeholder instead of aggregating the real `DeviceActivityData` hierarchy.

## User impact

Users no longer see a false promise that Eliza can read a coarse Screen Time summary in the host app. When Screen Time is authorized, the native report extension owns the privacy-preserving category presentation; unsupported or unavailable states remain explicit.

3b:Te14,Fixes #16994

## What

Makes the root package-test orchestrator (`packages/scripts/run-all-tests.mjs`) fail closed on empty or unobserved tasks via an exactly-once, machine-readable result contract:

- **Result ledger**: every resolved task produces exactly one record — `{label, packageName, relativeDir, scriptName, status: pass|skip|fail, observed, exitCode, signal, timedOut, durationMs, counts{tests, executed, failures, errors, skipped}, skipReason|failReason}` — printed as `[eliza-test] RESULT {...}` per task plus a `[eliza-test] RESULTS tasks=… pass=… fail=… skip=… excluded=… unobserved=…` summary, and written as JSON to `ELIZA_TEST_RESULTS_FILE` when set.
- **Exactly-once enforcement**: a green run with a missing or duplicate record for any resolved task is a protocol violation → exit 3. (These invariants cannot be violated from outside the process, so a documented `ELIZA_TEST_FAULT_INJECT=duplicate-record|drop-record` knob exists solely for the deterministic fault-injection tests.)
- **All-skipped guard**: under `--require-work`, a lane where every resolved task skipped exits 3 with a distinct diagnostic instead of relying only on the aggregate executed-tests floor.
- **Timeout**: `TEST_TASK_TIMEOUT_MS` (0 = off, validated) bounds each child; a timed-out child is killed (SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 10s) and recorded as a **failure** with `timedOut: true`, never a skip — even if it exits 0 during the grace window.
- **Explicit designed exclusions**: `--no-cloud` skips, empty-vitest-lane skips, and the pr-lane homepage e2e carve-out are recorded as `excluded` entries in both plan and run modes, mechanically distinct from passes.
- **Failure reporting**: serial fail-fast now flows through the same recorded-failure exit boundary as the pooled path (label + reason), instead of an unhandled top-level rejection.

Unobserved exit-0 wrappers keep their existing aggregate `--require-work` guard but are now individually marked `observed: false` in the ledger so downstream gates can reconcile per-task.

## Tests

New `packages/scripts/__tests__/run-all-tests-result-ledger.test.ts` spawns the **real runner** against temp workspace fixture packages (no mocks):

- passing task → exactly-once observed record + results file with counts
- all-skipped lane → exit 3, distinct guard text
- hung child + `TEST_TASK_TIMEOUT_MS=2000` → exit 1, `timedOut: true` record
- exit-0 zero-evidence wrapper → exit 3, `observed: false` record
- duplicate record (fault-injected) → exit 3
- dropped record (fault-injected) → exit 3
- malformed `TEST_TASK_TIMEOUT_MS` → exit 2 at validation
